- 2026-02-27T20:05Z [CODE] [plan:40-launcher-cwd-render-fix] Update launcher execution path from direct Bun source invocation to package-context invocation (`bun run --cwd <packages/opencode> --conditions=browser ./src/index.ts`) and inject caller directory as positional project only when no positional arg is present.

- 2026-03-07T10:53Z [CODE] [plan:48-python-review-tui] Implemented `--review-tui` as a thin TTY-only wrapper over the existing review queue, score cache, and ledger; it does not introduce new persistence or replace `--review-next`, and uses one-key actions relative to the current read/write suggestion.
- 2026-03-07T11:42Z [CODE] [plan:49-python-action-taxonomy-emit-pure] Planned taxonomy uses `read`, `write`, `emit`, `exec`, `pure`, and `unknown`; runtime behavior keeps `pure` analyzer-only (excluded from permission/review by default) while making `emit` first-class for analysis/review and non-blocking in permission asks.
- 2026-03-07T12:04Z [ASSUMPTION] [plan:49-python-action-taxonomy-emit-pure] 2026-03-07T12:30Z Proposed safest Phase 2 direction: permission asks are gated to read/write/exec/unknown only, python ask is skipped when no askable events remain, and emit/pure are retained only for observability metadata to avoid reducing existing safety coverage.

- 2026-03-08T12:02Z [CODE] [plan:50-python-analyzer-alias-effect-lattice] Phase 1 keeps `analyze()` outward-compatible (`PythonEvent[]` with current event kinds) while introducing only lexical alias normalization before rule matching; the richer internal effect lattice remains a documented design target for later phases rather than part of the live API.
- 2026-03-08T12:11Z [CODE] [plan:50-python-analyzer-alias-effect-lattice] Phase 2 documentation describes alias handling as a bounded lexical normalization pass: supported today are explicit import aliases, imported symbol aliases, same-scope local rebindings, and assignment-family invalidation, while non-assignment rebinding sites and wrapper/interprocedural summaries stay explicitly unsupported.
- 2026-03-08T12:22Z [CODE] [plan:50-python-analyzer-alias-effect-lattice] Phase 3 introduces internal `ResolvedEffectAtom` classification and fold-down mapping inside `src/python/python-analyze.ts`, preserving the outward `PythonEvent[]` API while separating resolved/canonical call identity from emitted call strings.
- 2026-03-08T12:37Z [CODE] [plan:50-python-analyzer-alias-effect-lattice] Phase 4 scope was narrowed to safe local callable-factory passthroughs only: zero-arg `def` bodies with a single direct `return <identifier|attribute>` and zero-arg assignment-time invocation can seed alias bindings, while parameterized, decorated, wrapper-style, and subscript-return factories stay unresolved.
- 2026-03-08T14:03Z [CODE] [plan:50-python-analyzer-alias-effect-lattice] Follow-up after plan 50 completion extends alias invalidation to common non-assignment rebinding sites only (`for`, `with ... as`, `except ... as`) as a delete-only safety boundary; it does not introduce new inference for those bindings.
- 2026-03-08T14:27Z [CODE] [plan:50-python-analyzer-alias-effect-lattice] Async rebinding support continues to use the same conservative delete-only model as sync rebinding, but invalidation now leaves a same-scope shadow barrier so inner rebinds stop resolving through parent aliases.
- 2026-03-08T14:50Z [CODE] [plan:49-python-action-taxonomy-emit-pure] Review TUI now opens in full-code mode by default while preserving `v` as a reversible toggle back to the focused-window view, keeping current-call highlighting and status behavior unchanged.
- 2026-03-08T15:21Z [CODE] [plan:51-python-external-io-semantics] Phase 1 narrows `write` to external mutation only: explicit HTTP helper verbs now split into read-like vs write-like buckets, while bounded local container methods (`append`, `update`, `add`, `get`, etc.) classify as `pure` only when the receiver is statically known to be a local list/dict/set.
- 2026-03-08T15:56Z [CODE] [plan:51-python-external-io-semantics] Phase 2 extends bounded external API coverage only for explicit known surfaces: direct imported HTTP helpers now resolve when the fully qualified target is a known network read/write/exec rule, and tracked client-method normalization applies only to direct `<instance>.<method>` calls on known constructors (`requests.Session`, `httpx.Client`, `httpx.AsyncClient`, `aiohttp.ClientSession`).
- 2026-03-08T17:09Z [CODE] [plan:51-python-external-io-semantics] Shadow invalidation for bounded direct-import helpers now runs through a shared definition path for plain functions, classes, and decorated definitions, but decorated definitions are scheduled at the inner def/class span so decorator call expressions still resolve against the pre-shadow binding first.
- 2026-03-08T18:13Z [CODE] [plan:49-python-action-taxonomy-emit-pure] Default `src/python-session-report.ts` review queues now include unresolved `unknown` callables only; `emit` requires explicit `--include-emit`, so promoted rule-backed emit callables stay out of default review runs while still remaining auditable on demand.
- 2026-03-08T18:43Z [CODE] [plan:52-python-http-request-verb-inference] Bounded `.request(method, ...)` inference now classifies only direct known HTTP surfaces plus tracked client `request`, `_transport.request`, and `_client._transport.request` chains when the method is a static literal; dynamic methods, unsupported verbs, and other nested/private chains stay `unknown`.
- 2026-03-08T21:30Z [CODE] [plan:53-python-json-container-purity] Plain `json.loads(...)` now classifies as `pure`, and direct `json.load(...)` / `json.loads(...)` assignments seed bounded local JSON-container provenance for dict/list-style methods; decoder hooks, parse hooks, and `**kwargs` disable that provenance and make customized `json.loads(...)` conservative `unknown`.
- 2026-03-08T21:57Z [CODE] [plan:54-python-session-history-provenance-audit] Session-history-driven follow-up now treats direct list/dict/set comprehensions as local containers for existing pure-method allowlists, and treats `dict.fromkeys` as pure only when builtin `dict` is not shadowed by local rebinding, parameters, or imports.
- 2026-03-08T22:46Z [CODE] [plan:55-python-response-json-provenance] 2026-03-08T23:45Z Tracked-response `.json()` purity/provenance is now gated by bounded HTTP-response instance tracking plus lexical shadow boundaries, so arbitrary or shadowed `.json()` receivers stay `unknown`.
- 2026-03-08T23:33Z [CODE] [plan:56-python-builtin-purity-strategy] Builtin pure-call coverage now uses lexical shadow guards plus callback guardrails for `max`, `min`, and `sorted`, and comprehension targets invalidate builtin names before body traversal to avoid false-pure results.
- 2026-03-08T23:33Z [CODE] [plan:57-python-standard-raise-classification] Standard builtin exception constructors and bare `raise <builtin-exception>` forms now classify as `pure` when unshadowed; shadowed names, custom exceptions, bare `raise`, and arbitrary raise expressions remain conservative.

- 2026-03-09T12:33Z [CODE] [plan:58-python-temporary-container-chain-purity] Direct temporary builtin/JSON receiver methods now reuse existing builtin and JSON guardrails, while local-container method purity is restricted to identifier receivers so subscript and attribute receiver methods do not get promoted accidentally.
- 2026-03-09T12:55Z [CODE] [plan:59-python-self-attribute-container-provenance] Same-scope `self.<attr>` container provenance now reuses guarded container-seeding logic and only resolves `self.*` container paths in the current lexical scope, preventing nested helper leakage.
- 2026-03-09T13:23Z [CODE] [plan:60-python-receiver-identity-provenance] Exact receiver-path provenance now tracks only same-scope direct one-hop attributes and identifier-based subscript receivers seeded from guarded container producers; richer forms like nested attributes or attribute-based subscript indices stay conservative.
- 2026-03-09T14:10Z [CODE] [plan:61-python-common-receiver-provenance] Common-case receiver provenance now supports only one-hop attribute index deps and shallow nested attribute receivers, with exact invalidation on matching one-hop attribute writes; deeper shapes stay conservative.

- 2026-03-07T13:57Z [TOOL] 2026-03-07T16:05Z [TOOL] In `src/python-session-report.ts`, `tui()` waits for `await rescore(...)` before repainting (`clear` + `renderTui`), so after a keypress there is no immediate visual acknowledgement during scorer latency; this is the primary source of perceived unresponsiveness.

- 2026-03-08T14:03Z [CODE] [plan:50-python-analyzer-alias-effect-lattice] `except_clause` invalidation must anchor from the `value` (`as_pattern`) boundary rather than the whole clause end, otherwise stale aliases can survive into handler-body calls; `with_item` and `for_statement` can be handled with source-order timeline anchors without widening inference.
- 2026-03-08T14:10Z [CODE] [plan:50-python-analyzer-alias-effect-lattice] Multi-item `with` correctness depends on invalidating at each `with_item` boundary in source order: a later header expression like `other(client.get(...))` must see `client` as rebound if an earlier item used `as client`, while nested function scopes must keep outer aliases intact.
- 2026-03-08T14:27Z [CODE] [plan:50-python-analyzer-alias-effect-lattice] Without a same-scope shadow barrier, rebinding inside an inner function can still resolve through a parent alias map (`client = requests` at module scope plus `async for client in ...` inside a function incorrectly classified `client.get(...)` as `requests.get`); setting the binding to its own name blocks that parent fallback while preserving unknown classification.

- 2026-03-08T15:21Z [CODE] [plan:51-python-external-io-semantics] The user-visible `changed.append(...)` false positive was coming from two layers: review scoring heuristics treated `append`/`update` as write-like names, and the analyzer lacked bounded local-container inference. A same-scope container tracker is enough to suppress local list/dict/set noise without adding unsafe generic method-name write rules.
- 2026-03-08T15:51Z [CODE] [plan:51-python-external-io-semantics] Tracked HTTP client method classification keys on tail method name whenever `parts.length >= 2`, so nested attributes like `client.api.get()` can be over-normalized to `requests.Session.get` instead of remaining unknown.
- 2026-03-08T15:51Z [CODE] [plan:51-python-external-io-semantics] Function definitions currently do not invalidate existing alias bindings (`definition` entries only register callable factories), so local `def get(...):` can leave prior imported alias bindings active for later `get(...)` calls.
- 2026-03-08T15:54Z [CODE] [plan:51-python-external-io-semantics] 2026-03-08T16:08Z Re-review confirms nested tracked HTTP client method normalization now requires a two-segment receiver shape (`parts.length === 2`), so chained receivers like `client.api.get()` stay conservative as unknown.
- 2026-03-08T15:54Z [CODE] [plan:51-python-external-io-semantics] 2026-03-08T16:08Z Direct-import shadowing fix invalidates alias bindings on `function_definition`, but class/decorated definitions are not added to definition invalidation entries, leaving a narrow potential shadowing gap for those forms.
- 2026-03-08T15:56Z [CODE] [plan:51-python-external-io-semantics] Two conservative guards were required to keep Phase 2 safe: tracked HTTP client normalization must only fire for exactly two call segments (`client.get`, not `client.api.get`), and local function definitions must invalidate imported helper bindings so `from requests import get` followed by `def get(...):` does not leak the imported `requests.get` identity.
- 2026-03-08T17:08Z [CODE] [plan:51-python-external-io-semantics] Class/decorated-definition shadowing hardening is wired through the generic definition timeline path (`definitionNode` + `definitionName` + `entry.kind === "definition"` invalidation), so `class_definition` and `decorated_definition` now clear imported-helper bindings before later calls resolve.
- 2026-03-08T17:09Z [CODE] [plan:51-python-external-io-semantics] The safe decorated-definition fix is timeline-based: if invalidation starts at the wrapper `@...` offset, decorator calls lose the imported helper too early; anchoring at the inner def/class span preserves `@get(...)` resolution while still shadowing later `get(...)` calls.
- 2026-03-08T17:14Z [CODE] [plan:51-python-external-io-semantics] The shared definition-timeline invalidation path already handled decorated classes correctly; the remaining gap was only missing regression coverage proving decorator-time resolution and post-definition shadowing in one snippet.
